          consequence, 9.9x the data written to the L2 cache has to be communicated to the L2 Compression unit. Try     
          maximizing local coherence for the write operations to compressible memory. For example, avoid writes with    
          large strides as they lead to partial accesses to many L2 cache lines. Instead, try accessing fewer overall   
          cache lines by modifying many values per cache line with each warp's execution of a write operation.

    OPT   Est. Speedup: 0.009611%                                                                                       
          Instructions are executed in warps, which are groups of 32 threads. Optimal instruction throughput is         
          achieved if all 32 threads of a warp execute the same instruction. The chosen launch configuration, early     
          thread completion, and divergent flow control can significantly lower the number of active threads in a warp  
          per cycle. This workload achieves an average of 32.0 threads being active per cycle. This is further reduced  
          to 17.2 threads per warp due to predication. The compiler may use predication to avoid an actual branch.      
          Instead, all instructions are scheduled, but a per-thread condition code or predicate controls which threads  
          execute the instructions. Try to avoid different execution paths within a warp when possible.

          This could possibly be caused by a stride between threads. Check the Source Counters section for uncoalesced  
          global stores.

          number of eligible warps, reduce the time the active warps are stalled by inspecting the top stall reasons

    OPT   Est. Speedup: 47.38%                                                                                          
          On average, each warp of this workload spends 9.9 cycles being stalled waiting to be selected to fetch an     
          instruction or waiting on an instruction cache miss. A high number of warps not having an instruction         
          fetched is typical for very short kernels with less than one full wave of work in the grid. Excessively       
          jumping across large blocks of assembly code can also lead to more warps stalled for this reason, if this     
          causes misses in the instruction cache. See also the related Branch Resolving state. This stall type          
          represents about 47.4% of the total average of 21.0 cycles between issuing two instructions.

          thread completion, and divergent flow control can significantly lower the number of active threads in a warp

          Instead, all instructions are scheduled, but a per-thread condition code or predicate controls which threads  
          execute the instructions. Try to avoid different execution paths within a warp when possible.

          consequence, 5.9x the data written to the L2 cache has to be communicated to the L2 Compression unit. Try     
          maximizing local coherence for the write operations to compressible memory. For example, avoid writes with    
          large strides as they lead to partial accesses to many L2 cache lines. Instead, try accessing fewer overall   
          cache lines by modifying many values per cache line with each warp's execution of a write operation.

    Section: Warp State Statistics
    ---------------------------------------- ----------- ------------
    Metric Name                              Metric Unit Metric Value
    ---------------------------------------- ----------- ------------
    Warp Cycles Per Issued Instruction             cycle        12.07
    Warp Cycles Per Executed Instruction           cycle        12.20
    Avg. Active Threads Per Warp                                   32
    Avg. Not Predicated Off Threads Per Warp                    20.43
    ---------------------------------------- ----------- ------------

    OPT   Est. Speedup: 38.92%                                                                                          
          On average, each warp of this workload spends 4.7 cycles being stalled waiting for a scoreboard dependency on 
          a L1TEX (local, global, surface, texture) operation. Find the instruction producing the data being waited     
          upon to identify the culprit. To reduce the number of cycles waiting on L1TEX data accesses verify the        
          memory access patterns are optimal for the target architecture, attempt to increase cache hit rates by        
          increasing data locality (coalescing), or by changing the cache configuration. Consider moving frequently     
          used data to shared memory. This stall type represents about 38.9% of the total average of 12.1 cycles        
          between issuing two instructions.

          thread completion, and divergent flow control can significantly lower the number of active threads in a warp  
          per cycle. This workload achieves an average of 32.0 threads being active per cycle. This is further reduced  
          to 20.4 threads per warp due to predication. The compiler may use predication to avoid an actual branch.      
          Instead, all instructions are scheduled, but a per-thread condition code or predicate controls which threads  
          execute the instructions. Try to avoid different execution paths within a warp when possible.

          cache lines by modifying many values per cache line with each warp's execution of a write operation.

          large strides as they lead to partial accesses to many L2 cache lines. Instead, try accessing fewer overall   
          cache lines by modifying many values per cache line with each warp's execution of a write operation.

          number of eligible warps, reduce the time the active warps are stalled by inspecting the top stall reasons

          per cycle. This workload achieves an average of 32.0 threads being active per cycle. This is further reduced  
          to 20.4 threads per warp due to predication. The compiler may use predication to avoid an actual branch.      
          Instead, all instructions are scheduled, but a per-thread condition code or predicate controls which threads  
          execute the instructions. Try to avoid different execution paths within a warp when possible.

    OPT   Est. Speedup: 0.07%                                                                                           
          Out of the 5888.0 bytes sent to the L2 Compression unit only 0.00% were successfully compressed. To increase  
          this success rate, consider marking only those memory regions as compressible that contain the most zero      
          values and/or expose the most homogeneous values.                                                             
    ----- --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    OPT   Est. Speedup: 0.07%                                                                                           
          The access patterns for writes to compressible memory are not well suited for the L2 Compression unit. As a   
          consequence, 5.8x the data written to the L2 cache has to be communicated to the L2 Compression unit. Try     
          maximizing local coherence for the write operations to compressible memory. For example, avoid writes with    
          large strides as they lead to partial accesses to many L2 cache lines. Instead, try accessing fewer overall   
          cache lines by modifying many values per cache line with each warp's execution of a write operation.

    OPT   Est. Speedup: 0.06981%                                                                                        
          The access patterns for writes to compressible memory are not well suited for the L2 Compression unit. As a   
          consequence, 6.2x the data written to the L2 cache has to be communicated to the L2 Compression unit. Try     
          maximizing local coherence for the write operations to compressible memory. For example, avoid writes with    
          large strides as they lead to partial accesses to many L2 cache lines. Instead, try accessing fewer overall   
          cache lines by modifying many values per cache line with each warp's execution of a write operation.

    OPT   Est. Speedup: 0.05869%                                                                                        
          The access patterns for writes to compressible memory are not well suited for the L2 Compression unit. As a   
          consequence, 5.3x the data written to the L2 cache has to be communicated to the L2 Compression unit. Try     
          maximizing local coherence for the write operations to compressible memory. For example, avoid writes with    
          large strides as they lead to partial accesses to many L2 cache lines. Instead, try accessing fewer overall   
          cache lines by modifying many values per cache line with each warp's execution of a write operation.

          Out of the 5888.0 bytes sent to the L2 Compression unit only 0.00% were successfully compressed. To increase  
          this success rate, consider marking only those memory regions as compressible that contain the most zero      
          values and/or expose the most homogeneous values.                                                             
    ----- --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    OPT   Est. Speedup: 0.07004%                                                                                        
          The access patterns for writes to compressible memory are not well suited for the L2 Compression unit. As a   
          consequence, 5.8x the data written to the L2 cache has to be communicated to the L2 Compression unit. Try     
          maximizing local coherence for the write operations to compressible memory. For example, avoid writes with    
          large strides as they lead to partial accesses to many L2 cache lines. Instead, try accessing fewer overall   
          cache lines by modifying many values per cache line with each warp's execution of a write operation.

          of 1.11 active warps per scheduler, but only an average of 0.08 warps were eligible per cycle. Eligible       
          warps are the subset of active warps that are ready to issue their next instruction. Every cycle with no      
          eligible warp results in no instruction being issued and the issue slot remains unused. To increase the       
          number of eligible warps, reduce the time the active warps are stalled by inspecting the top stall reasons

    OPT   Est. Speedup: 0.06083%                                                                                        
          The access patterns for writes to compressible memory are not well suited for the L2 Compression unit. As a   
          consequence, 5.9x the data written to the L2 cache has to be communicated to the L2 Compression unit. Try     
          maximizing local coherence for the write operations to compressible memory. For example, avoid writes with    
          large strides as they lead to partial accesses to many L2 cache lines. Instead, try accessing fewer overall   
          cache lines by modifying many values per cache line with each warp's execution of a write operation.

          thread completion, and divergent flow control can significantly lower the number of active threads in a warp  
          per cycle. This workload achieves an average of 32.0 threads being active per cycle. This is further reduced  
          to 20.4 threads per warp due to predication. The compiler may use predication to avoid an actual branch.      
          Instead, all instructions are scheduled, but a per-thread condition code or predicate controls which threads  
          execute the instructions. Try to avoid different execution paths within a warp when possible.

    ----- --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    INF   Check the Warp Stall Sampling (All Samples) table for the top stall locations in your source based on         
          sampling data. The Profiling Guide                                                                            
          (https://docs.nvidia.com/nsight-compute/ProfilingGuide/index.html#metrics-reference) provides more details    
          on each stall reason.                                                                                         
    ----- --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    OPT   Est. Speedup: 0.008076%                                                                                       
          Instructions are executed in warps, which are groups of 32 threads. Optimal instruction throughput is         
          achieved if all 32 threads of a warp execute the same instruction. The chosen launch configuration, early     
          thread completion, and divergent flow control can significantly lower the number of active threads in a warp  
          per cycle. This workload achieves an average of 32.0 threads being active per cycle. This is further reduced  
          to 20.4 threads per warp due to predication. The compiler may use predication to avoid an actual branch.      
          Instead, all instructions are scheduled, but a per-thread condition code or predicate controls which threads  
          execute the instructions. Try to avoid different execution paths within a warp when possible.

          higher-throughput equivalent, the achieved FP64 performance could be increased by up to 50% (relative to its
